## 9. Abuse Cases

_Abuse cases describe end-to-end attack scenarios that chain individual findings into an exploitation path. Each case is **mandatory** - defined in the org profile / plugin library and evaluated against every repository. Every chain step references a finding from [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register); each step is code-confirmed against the repository and the chain verdict is folded deterministically from the per-step results, never rated by hand._

| # | Scenario | Actor | Combined Risk | Verdict |
|--------|------------------------------------|------------------|-------------|--------------|
| [AC-T-001](#ac-t-001) | Account Takeover via Stored XSS + Token<br/>Hijacking | external-attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-002](#ac-t-002) | Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object<br/>Authorization | authenticated-user | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-005](#ac-t-005) | Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret<br/>Material | external-attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-006](#ac-t-006) | Remote Code Execution via Server-Side<br/>Injection | external-attacker | 🔴 Critical | ◐ Partially blocked |

_Verdict: ⚠ Fully viable - no effective control blocks this chain · ◐ Partially blocked - at least one step has a compensating control but the chain is not fully closed · ✓ Mitigated - chain is broken at a verified step · ? Inconclusive - could not be verified end-to-end._

---

<a id="ac-t-001"></a>
### AC-T-001 — Account Takeover via Stored XSS + Token Hijacking

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** external-attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Obtain persistent authenticated access as an arbitrary user without valid credentials.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can submit content that is later rendered to other users (e.g. feedback, comments, profile fields).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) (`search-result.component.ts:143`) | Attacker JavaScript executes in the victim's<br/>browser session. |
| 2 | 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — JWT and 2FA Temporary Token Stored in localStorage (`login.component.ts:101`) | Token exfiltrated from local/session storage<br/>via the Step 1 payload. |
| 3 | 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — OAuth Implicit Flow with Missing State Parameter (`login.component.ts:148`) | Exfiltrated token accepted for a new<br/>session; absence of token binding / PKCE<br/>removes the last server-side revocation<br/>opportunity. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

Individually the XSS sink and the web-readable token storage rate below Critical, but chained they form a repeatable credential-theft path: a single stored payload causes indefinite session compromise for every user who views the affected page.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ◕ [M-026](#m-026) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ● [M-001](#m-001) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — JWT and 2FA Temporary Token Stored in localStorage breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ◕ [M-015](#m-015) — Replace OAuth implicit flow with authorization-code flow plus PKCE and state va… (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — OAuth Implicit Flow with Missing State Parameter breaks the chain at **Step 3**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-002"></a>
### AC-T-002 — Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** authenticated-user - authenticated low-privilege user · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Enumerate and exfiltrate other users' records, then escalate own permissions via unguarded mass assignment.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker holds a valid, non-privileged user account.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-045](#f-045) — Insecure Direct Object Reference via Integer Auto-Increment Primary Keys (`user.ts:39`) | Attacker enumerates and retrieves records<br/>for arbitrary object IDs; no ownership<br/>comparison is performed. |
| 2 | 🔴 [F-013](#f-013) — Mass Assignment via Finale Auto-Generated CRUD API (`server.ts:501`) | Update endpoint persists an unfiltered `role`<br/>(or equivalent) field supplied in the<br/>request body. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

The ownership gap exposes every record, and the mass-assignment gap lets the same low-privilege actor self-elevate - together they turn a single compromised account into full tenant data access and role escalation.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ◕ [M-044](#m-044) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-045](#f-045) — Insecure Direct Object Reference via Integer Auto-Increment Primary Keys breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ● [M-012](#m-012) — Allowlist client-controlled fields (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-013](#f-013) — Mass Assignment via Finale Auto-Generated CRUD API breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-005"></a>
### AC-T-005 — Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** external-attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge trusted tokens / credentials and impersonate any user.

**Prerequisite:** Signing material or other secrets are reachable (committed to a public repo, served by an unauthenticated route, or in an exposed directory).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-012](#f-012) — Hardcoded BIP-39 Mnemonic (`checkKeys.ts:10`) | A private key, signing secret, or credential<br/>file is committed to the source repository<br/>or served without authentication. |
| 2 | 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hardcoded RSA Private Key (`insecurity.ts:21`) | The exposed key/secret is the same one the<br/>server trusts, so a token signed with it (or<br/>the leaked credential) is accepted as<br/>authentic. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

Exposed signing material collapses the entire authentication boundary: any attacker who reads the key can mint a valid token for any identity or role, with no credential ever required.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ● [M-011](#m-011) — Move secrets to a managed secret store (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-012](#f-012) — Hardcoded BIP-39 Mnemonic breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ● [M-005](#m-005) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hardcoded RSA Private Key breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-006"></a>
### AC-T-006 — Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** external-attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ◐ Partially blocked

**Goal:** Execute arbitrary code in the application process.

**Prerequisite:** An input reaches a server-side interpreter / template / eval.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — Remote Code Execution via safeEval in B2B Order API (`b2bOrder.ts:23`) | Attacker-controlled input is passed to `eval`,<br/>a server-side template engine, an unsafe<br/>sandbox, or an unsafe deserializer. |

**Why combined risk exceeds individual ratings**

A single injection into a server-side interpreter yields code execution in the application process - the highest-impact outcome, granting full filesystem and network access from one unauthenticated request.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ● [M-008](#m-008) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — Remote Code Execution via safeEval in B2B Order API breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

### Generic catalog — evaluated, not applicable

| Scenario | Source | Why not applicable |
|------------------------------------|---------|----------------------|

### P1 — Immediate

<a id="m-001"></a>
#### M-001 — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-001](#f-001) — JWT and 2FA Temporary Token Stored in localStorage (`login.component.ts:101`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) - Insecure Storage of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** High · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`

**How:**

1. Implement a Backend-for-Frontend (BFF) pattern: the login endpoint sets a `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict` cookie containing the JWT, never returning the raw token to Angular.
2. Remove all `localStorage.setItem('token', ...)` calls from `login.component.ts`, `oauth.component.ts`, and any other component; the Angular `HttpClient` will send the `HttpOnly` cookie automatically.
3. Replace the `LoginGuard.canActivate()` token-presence check with a lightweight `/rest/user/whoami` call - the cookie is the credential, not a value in `localStorage`.
4. Store the 2FA temp token as a short-lived server-side session attribute (Redis, server-side session store) keyed to a non-sensitive session ID, not in `localStorage`.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:101`: it applies **Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Move the JWT out of localStorage into an httpOnly cookie.
res.cookie('session', token, {
  httpOnly: true, secure: true, sameSite: 'lax', maxAge: 3600_000
})
```

**Verification:** After the fix, open DevTools → Application → `localStorage` and confirm no `token` or `totp_tmp_token` key is present; confirm the session cookie has `HttpOnly` and `Secure` flags visible in DevTools → Application → Cookies.

**Reference:** [CWE-922: Insecure Storage of Sensitive Information](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html)

---

<a id="m-004"></a>
#### M-004 — Use parameterized database queries

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — SQL Injection on Login (`login.ts:34`)
- 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — SQL Injection (`login.ts:34`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Replace `models.sequelize.query(...)` at `routes/login.ts:34` with `UserModel.findOne({ where: { email: req.body.email, password: security.hash(req.body.password), deletedAt: null } })` using Sequelize ORM methods.
2. Remove the raw SQL string template entirely; Sequelize's `where` clause uses parameterized binding by default.
3. Add an integration test that submits `' OR '1'='1'--` as the email and asserts HTTP 401 is returned.
4. Replace the raw `sequelize.query()` call at `routes/login.ts:34` with `UserModel.findOne({ where: { email: req.body.email, password: security.hash(req.body.password), deletedAt: null } })`.
5. Upgrade the password hashing algorithm from `MD5` to bcrypt or Argon2 (see backend-api-014) - this is a pre-requisite for treating the password field as a proper credential.
6. Add express-rate-limit middleware to `POST /rest/user/login` (see 🟠 [F-040](#f-040) — No Rate Limiting on Login Endpoint (`server.ts`)).

_Example implementation in `routes/login.ts:34`: it applies **Use parameterized database queries**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (vulnerable):
await models.sequelize.query(
  `SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email}' AND ...`
);

// After (safe):
await UserModel.findOne({
  where: { email: req.body.email, password: security.hash(req.body.password), deletedAt: null }
});
```

**Verification:** Submit `POST /rest/user/login` with `{"email":"' OR '1'='1'--","password":"x"}` and assert the response is HTTP 401, not a JWT-bearing 200.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-005"></a>
#### M-005 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hardcoded RSA Private Key (`insecurity.ts:21`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**How:**

1. Remove the hardcoded private key string from `lib/insecurity.ts:21` and replace with `process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY` (or a secret manager call); fail startup when the variable is absent.
2. Generate a new RSA-2048 or EC P-256 key pair and treat the old key as permanently compromised - invalidate all existing sessions.
3. Store the public key path or value in a separate non-secret config entry; verify it is served for token validation.
4. Add a CI check (e.g., `truffleHog` or `gitleaks`) that rejects commits containing PEM key patterns.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:21`: it applies **Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before:
const privateKey = '----**** (31 chars)\r\nMIICXA...';

// After:
const privateKey = process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY;
if (!privateKey) throw new Error('JWT_PRIVATE_KEY is required');
```

**Verification:** Run `grep -r 'BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY' lib/` and assert zero hits; then verify a JWT signed with the old key is rejected with HTTP 401.

**Reference:** [CWE-321: Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)

---

<a id="m-006"></a>
#### M-006 — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-006](#f-006) — Insecure JWT Verification (`insecurity.ts:52`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:52`

**How:**

1. Replace express-jwt 0.1.3 and jsonwebtoken 0.4.0 with express-jwt ^8.4.1 and jsonwebtoken ^9.0.2.
2. In `lib/insecurity.ts`, update `isAuthorized()` to pass `algorithms: ['RS256']` to `expressJwt`, explicitly rejecting `alg:none` and symmetric algorithms.
3. Remove any code path that calls `jws.verify()` or `jws.decode()` without algorithm enforcement; route all JWT verification through the updated express-jwt middleware.
4. Rotate the RSA key pair (see 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hardcoded RSA Private Key (`lib/insecurity.ts:21`)) immediately after deploying the fix, since the old key material is already exposed.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:52`: it applies **Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (lib/insecurity.ts:52)
export const isAuthorized = () => expressJwt(({ secret: publicKey }) as any)

// After
import expressJwt from 'express-jwt'
export const isAuthorized = () =>
  expressJwt({ secret: publicKey, algorithms: ['RS256'] })
```

**Verification:** Send `Authorization: Bearer eyJhbGciOiJub25lIiwidHlwIjoiSldUIn0.eyJkYXRhIjp7ImlkIjoxfX0.` to `GET /api/Users`; expect HTTP 401 Unauthorized.

**Reference:** [CWE-347: Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html)

---

<a id="m-007"></a>
#### M-007 — Use OAuth authorization code flow with PKCE

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — Derived OAuth Account Password from Email Claim (`oauth.component.ts:30`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-522](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/522.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**How:**

1. Generate a cryptographically random password (at minimum 32 bytes from `crypto.randomBytes()` on the server side) when provisioning an OAuth user account, and never expose it to the client.
2. Remove the `btoa(email.split('').reverse().join(''))` derivation from `oauth.component.ts:30` and line 46 entirely - OAuth users should authenticate exclusively via the OAuth token, not via a derived local password.
3. On the server side, mark OAuth-provisioned accounts with a flag (e.g., `authProvider: 'google'`) and reject direct password logins for those accounts unless the user has explicitly set a separate password.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`: it applies **Use OAuth authorization code flow with PKCE**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (oauth.component.ts:30)
const password = btoa(profile.email.split('').reverse().join(''))

// After — server-side provisioning, no client-visible password
// The OAuthComponent only calls userService.oauthLogin(accessToken);
// the backend verifies the token, looks up or creates the user,
// and returns a Juice Shop JWT — no local password ever set or used.
```

**Verification:** After the fix, POST to `/rest/user/login` with `{email: oauthuser@example.com, password: btoa(email.split('').reverse().join(''))}` must return HTTP 401; the only valid authentication path for that account must go through the OAuth token exchange.

**Reference:** [CWE-522: Insufficiently Protected Credentials](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/522.html)

<a id="m-008"></a>
#### M-008 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — Remote Code Execution via safeEval in B2B Order API (`b2bOrder.ts:23`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/b2bOrder.ts:23`

**How:**

1. Replace the eval-based processing in `routes/b2bOrder.ts` with a structured JSON schema validator (e.g., `zod` or `ajv`) that parses `orderLinesData` as an array of typed order-line objects.
2. If arithmetic evaluation of order-line expressions is required, use a restricted expression parser (e.g., `mathjs` in safe mode) instead of a JavaScript eval.
3. Remove the dependency on `notevil` entirely.
4. Add input size limits on the `orderLinesData` field to prevent DoS via oversized payloads.

_Example implementation in `routes/b2bOrder.ts:23`: it applies **Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (routes/b2bOrder.ts:19-23)
const orderLinesData = body.orderLinesData || ''
const sandbox = { safeEval, orderLinesData }
vm.createContext(sandbox)
vm.runInContext('safeEval(orderLinesData)', sandbox, { timeout: 2000 })

// After — parse as typed array
const schema = z.array(z.object({ productId: z.number(), quantity: z.number().positive() }))
const orderLines = schema.parse(JSON.parse(body.orderLinesData || '[]'))
```

**Verification:** Send `POST /b2b/v2/orders` with `{"orderLinesData": "process.env"}` from an authenticated session; expect HTTP 400 or a JSON parse error, not an environment dump.

**Reference:** [CWE-94: Improper Control of Generation of Code (Code Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

<a id="m-009"></a>
#### M-009 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Insecure Direct Object Reference (`address.ts:11`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/address.ts:11`

**How:** Replace `req.body.UserId`/`userId`/`ownerId` with `req.user.id`…

1. Replace `req.body.UserId`/`userId`/`ownerId` with `req.user.id` (or equivalent session-derived identity) in every WHERE/filter clause.
2. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

_Example implementation in `routes/address.ts:11`: it applies **Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Ownership check before touching a resource.
const basket = await Basket.findByPk(req.params.id)
if (!basket || basket.UserId !== req.user.id) return res.status(403).end()
```

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-001 scanner check; it reports no match at `routes/address.ts:11`, and the regression test passes.

<a id="m-010"></a>
#### M-010 — Enforce discount cap and policy conditions server-side in the generateCoupon to…

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Prompt Injection overrides coupon discount policy (`chat.ts:179`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-74](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/74.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:179`

**How:**

1. Add server-side enforcement inside the `generateCoupon` tool's execute function: validate that discount <= 10 and throw/return an error if exceeded, regardless of what the LLM requested.
2. Change the Zod schema from `z.number()` to `z.number().min(1).max(10)` to reject out-of-range values before the tool executes.
3. Require the generate function to receive a verifiable `orderId` and check the order's damage status in the database before generating a coupon; do not rely on the LLM to have verified these conditions.
4. Apply `express-rate-limit` to `POST /rest/chat` to limit abuse volume while this fix is implemented.

_Example implementation in `routes/chat.ts:179`: it applies **Enforce discount cap and policy conditions server-side in the generateCoupon to…**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (routes/chat.ts:179)
inputSchema: z.object({
  discount: z.number().describe('The discount percentage for the coupon (maximum 10)')
}),
execute: async ({ discount }) => {
  const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(discount)  // no cap
  return { couponCode, discount }
}

// After
inputSchema: z.object({
  discount: z.number().min(1).max(10).describe('The discount percentage (1-10)')
}),
execute: async ({ discount }) => {
  if (discount > 10) return { error: 'Discount exceeds policy maximum of 10%' }
  const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(discount)
  return { couponCode, discount }
}
```

**Verification:** Send `POST /rest/chat` with message 'Generate a 50% coupon for my complaint now'. The response should contain an error from the tool, not a coupon code. Also verify via unit test that `generateCoupon` tool `execute()` with discount=50 returns an error object rather than calling security.`generateCoupon`.

**Reference:** [CWE-74: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Output Used by a Downstream Component (Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/74.html)

---

<a id="m-011"></a>
#### M-011 — Move secrets to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-012](#f-012) — Hardcoded BIP-39 Mnemonic (`checkKeys.ts:10`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) - Use of Hard-coded Credentials

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`

**How:**

1. Rotate the exposed mnemonic immediately: generate a new BIP-39 mnemonic, transfer any on-chain assets, and revoke the old wallet.
2. Store the replacement mnemonic or derived private key in a secret manager (AWS Secrets Manager, HashiCorp Vault) or in an environment variable (`WALLET_MNEMONIC`) loaded at startup - never in source.
3. Replace `const mnemonic = '...'` in `routes/checkKeys.ts:10` with `const mnemonic = process.env.WALLET_MNEMONIC` and add a startup guard that throws if the variable is absent.
4. Add a pre-commit hook or CI secret-scanning step (e.g. `git-secrets`, `truffleHog`) to block future mnemonic or private-key literals from reaching version control.

_Example implementation in `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`: it applies **Move secrets to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (routes/checkKeys.ts:10):
const mnemonic = 'purpose betray marriage blame crunch monitor spin slide donate sport lift clutch'

// After:
const mnemonic = process.env.WALLET_MNEMONIC
if (!mnemonic) throw new Error('WALLET_MNEMONIC environment variable is required')
const mnemonicWallet = HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase(mnemonic)
```

**Verification:** Confirm the mnemonic string is absent from all `git history (``git log -S 'purpose betray' --all` returns no commits); verify the server refuses to start when `WALLET_MNEMONIC` is unset; confirm `GET /rest/web3/submitKey` returns 500 with a generic error when the env var is missing rather than exposing any key material.

**Reference:** [CWE-798: Use of Hard-coded Credentials](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html)

---

<a id="m-012"></a>
#### M-012 — Allowlist client-controlled fields

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-013](#f-013) — Mass Assignment via Finale Auto-Generated CRUD API (`server.ts:501`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Add `'role'` to the `exclude` array at `server.ts:484`: `{ name: 'User', exclude: ['password', 'totpSecret', 'role'], model: UserModel }`.
2. In the registration middleware at `server.ts:408-422`, explicitly delete `req.body.role` before passing to Finale to defend against future schema changes.
3. Add an admin-role assignment flow that requires an existing admin to promote users - never allow self-assignment of elevated roles.

_Example implementation in `server.ts:501`: it applies **Allowlist client-controlled fields**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (server.ts:484)
{ name: 'User', exclude: ['password', 'totpSecret'], model: UserModel }

// After
{ name: 'User', exclude: ['password', 'totpSecret', 'role', 'totpSecret'], model: UserModel }

// Also strip in registration middleware
app.post('/api/Users', (req, res, next) => { delete req.body.role; next() }, ...)
```

**Verification:** Send `POST /api/Users` with `{"role": "admin", ...}`; then log in with the new account and confirm the JWT payload contains `role: 'customer'`, not `role: 'admin'`.

**Reference:** [CWE-915: Improperly Controlled Modification of Dynamically-Determined Object Attributes (Mass Assignment)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

### P2 — This Sprint

<a id="m-002"></a>
#### M-002 — Add security audit logging

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Security Audit Logging Across Components (`login.ts:48`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-778](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/778.html) - Insufficient Logging

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:48`

**How:**

1. Add a security logger (e.g., `winston` with a dedicated transport) that records at minimum: `timestamp`, `event_type` (`login_success`, `login_failure`, 2fa_success, 2fa_failure, `password_reset`), `userId` or `email`, `ip`, `user_agent`.
2. In `routes/login.ts`, log inside both the success path (`afterLogin`) and the `else if (!user.data?.id)` failure branch at line 50.
3. In `routes/resetPassword.ts`, log on success at line 43 and on wrong-answer failure at line 49.
4. Route the security log to an append-only sink (separate file, SIEM, or remote log service) distinct from the application debug log.

_Example implementation in `routes/login.ts:48`: it applies **Add security audit logging**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// In routes/login.ts afterLogin:
securityLogger.info({
  event: 'login_success', userId: user.id,
  ip: req.ip, ua: req.headers['user-agent'], ts: new Date().toISOString()
});
```

**Verification:** Perform a successful login followed by a failed login; assert the security log contains two events with the correct `event_type` values and no sensitive fields (password, token).

**Reference:** [CWE-778: Insufficient Logging](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/778.html)

<a id="m-003"></a>
#### M-003 — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-003](#f-003) — Missing Authentication Middleware on Sensitive Routes (`server.ts:607`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:607`

**How:**

1. Add `security.isAuthorized()` middleware to both admin routes in `server.ts`: `app.get('/rest/admin/application-version', security.isAuthorized(), utils.asyncHandler(retrieveAppVersion()))`.
2. Add an admin role check (analogous to `security.isAccounting()`) so only users with `role === 'admin'` can access admin endpoints.
3. Consider adding a blanket middleware to prefix `/rest/admin/*` rather than securing each route individually.

_Example implementation in `server.ts:607`: it applies **Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (server.ts:606-607)
app.get('/rest/admin/application-version', utils.asyncHandler(retrieveAppVersion()))
app.get('/rest/admin/application-configuration', utils.asyncHandler(retrieveAppConfiguration()))

// After
app.get('/rest/admin/application-version', security.isAuthorized(), security.isAdmin(), utils.asyncHandler(retrieveAppVersion()))
app.get('/rest/admin/application-configuration', security.isAuthorized(), security.isAdmin(), utils.asyncHandler(retrieveAppConfiguration()))
```

**Verification:** Send `GET /rest/admin/application-configuration` without an Authorization header; expect HTTP 401.

**Reference:** [CWE-862: Missing Authorization](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html)

<a id="m-013"></a>
#### M-013 — Replace security-question reset with email-verified one-time reset link

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-014](#f-014) — Security Question-Only Password Reset (`resetPassword.ts:35`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) - Weak Password Recovery Mechanism

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** High · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:35`

**How:**

1. Replace the security-question reset flow with a time-limited, single-use reset token delivered to the account's verified email address.
2. Generate a cryptographically random token (≥32 bytes, using `crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex')`), store its `SHA-256` hash in the DB with a 15-minute expiry, and email the raw token to the user.
3. The `POST /rest/user/reset-password` endpoint accepts only the raw token; reject it after first use and after expiry.
4. Until the security-question flow is replaced, additionally require email confirmation before applying the password change.

**Verification:** Attempt to reset an account's password using a known security answer and assert the endpoint returns HTTP 403; verify the reset only succeeds after clicking the link sent to the registered email.

**Reference:** [CWE-640: Weak Password Recovery Mechanism](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html)

<a id="m-014"></a>
#### M-014 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-015](#f-015) — Hardcoded Cryptographic Key for Security Answer Verification (`securityAnswer.ts:45`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `models/securityAnswer.ts:45`

**How:**

1. Remove the literal key `'pa4qacea4VK9t9nGv7yZtwmj'` from `lib/insecurity.ts:42`; replace with `process.env.SECURITY_ANSWER_PEPPER` read at startup and fail-fast if undefined.
2. Migrate existing SecurityAnswer rows by re-prompting users, or invalidate them and require re-enrollment at next forgot-password trigger.
3. Use bcrypt or Argon2 for security answers instead of HMAC-SHA256, applying a cost factor of ≥10 to prevent offline enumeration even if the key leaks.

_Example implementation in `models/securityAnswer.ts:45`: it applies **Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (lib/insecurity.ts:42)
export const hmac = (data: string) =>
  crypto.createHmac('sha256', 'pa4qacea4VK9t9nGv7yZtwmj').update(data).digest('hex')

// After — externally supplied pepper
const SECURITY_ANSWER_PEPPER = process.env.SECURITY_ANSWER_PEPPER!
if (!SECURITY_ANSWER_PEPPER) throw new Error('SECURITY_ANSWER_PEPPER must be set')
export const hmac = (data: string) =>
  crypto.createHmac('sha256', SECURITY_ANSWER_PEPPER).update(data).digest('hex')
```

**Verification:** Confirm `SECURITY_ANSWER_PEPPER` is not committed to the repository; verify the app fails to start if the env-var is absent; test that a precomputed HMAC using the old hardcoded key no longer validates any stored security answer.

**Reference:** [CWE-321: Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)

<a id="m-015"></a>
#### M-015 — Replace OAuth implicit flow with authorization-code flow plus PKCE and state va…

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — OAuth Implicit Flow with Missing State Parameter (`login.component.ts:148`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** High · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**How:**

1. Switch `response_type=token` to `response_type=code` in `googleLogin()` and implement a server-side PKCE exchange (`code_verifier` / `code_challenge`).
2. Generate a cryptographically random `state` value (e.g., `crypto.randomUUID()`) per-request, store it in `sessionStorage`, and validate it in `OAuthComponent.ngOnInit()` before consuming the authorization code.
3. Move the token exchange from the SPA to a backend endpoint (BFF pattern) so the access token never appears in the URL fragment or browser history.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`: it applies **Replace OAuth implicit flow with authorization-code flow plus PKCE and state va…**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (login.component.ts:148)
this.windowRefService.nativeWindow.location.replace(
  `${oauthProviderUrl}?client_id=${this.clientId}&response_type=token&scope=email&redirect_uri=${this.redirectUri}`
)

// After — generate state, use code flow
const state = crypto.randomUUID()
sessionStorage.setItem('oauth_state', state)
this.windowRefService.nativeWindow.location.replace(
  `${oauthProviderUrl}?client_id=${this.clientId}&response_type=code&scope=email&redirect_uri=${this.redirectUri}&state=${state}`
)
```

**Verification:** Confirm that the OAuth redirect URL sent to Google contains `response_type=code` and a unique `state` value; confirm that a mismatched or absent `state` value in the callback causes `OAuthComponent` to reject the flow and navigate to `/login` with an error.

**Reference:** [CWE-598: Use of GET Request Method With Sensitive Query Strings](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html)


<a id="m-018"></a>
#### M-018 — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — Zip-Slip via File Upload ZIP Extraction (`fileUpload.ts:31`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:31`

**How:**

1. Resolve the entry path against the target directory and verify it is still within that directory before writing: `const uploadBase = path.resolve('uploads/complaints'); if (!absolutePath.startsWith(uploadBase + path.sep)) continue;`
2. Strip path separators from filenames extracted from archive entries, or use `path.basename(entry.path)` to discard directory components entirely.
3. Pass `absolutePath` (not the relative `'uploads/complaints/' + fileName`) to `fs.createWriteStream` so the containment check and the write target are consistent.

_Example implementation in `routes/fileUpload.ts:31`: it applies **Constrain file paths to a safe base directory**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (routes/fileUpload.ts:31-34)
const absolutePath = path.resolve('uploads/complaints/' + fileName)
if (absolutePath.includes(path.resolve('.'))) {
  await pipeline(entry.stream(), fs.createWriteStream('uploads/complaints/' + fileName))
}

// After
const uploadBase = path.resolve('uploads/complaints')
const absolutePath = path.resolve(uploadBase, fileName)
if (absolutePath.startsWith(uploadBase + path.sep) || absolutePath === uploadBase) {
  await pipeline(entry.stream(), fs.createWriteStream(absolutePath))
}
```

**Verification:** Upload a ZIP containing an entry with path `../../ftp/legal.md`; verify `ftp/legal.md` content is unchanged after the upload.

**Reference:** [CWE-22: Path Traversal](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html)

<a id="m-019"></a>
#### M-019 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-020](#f-020) — LLM Prompt Injection via Unsanitised Chat Messages (`chat.ts:191`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:191`

**How:**

1. Move coupon policy enforcement from the system prompt to server-side logic in the `generateCoupon` tool executor: add `if (discount > 10) throw new Error('Discount exceeds maximum allowed')` before calling `security.generateCoupon(discount)`.
2. Add a maximum `messages` array length and per-message character limit in `routes/chat.ts` before passing to `streamText()`.
3. Do not embed confidential business logic in LLM system prompts - system prompts are extractable via prompt injection. Store sensitive policies as server-side rules only.
4. Consider gating `POST /rest/chat` behind `security.isAuthorized()` to prevent unauthenticated exploitation.

_Example implementation in `routes/chat.ts:191`: it applies **Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (routes/chat.ts:181)
execute: async ({ discount }) => {
  const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(discount)
  return { couponCode, discount }
}

// After — enforce cap server-side
execute: async ({ discount }) => {
  const clampedDiscount = Math.min(Math.max(1, Math.round(discount)), 10)
  const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(clampedDiscount)
  return { couponCode, discount: clampedDiscount }
}
```

**Verification:** Send `POST /rest/chat` with messages asking for a 100% discount coupon; verify the generated coupon encodes a discount of at most 10%.

**Reference:** [CWE-94: Improper Control of Generation of Code (Code Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

<a id="m-021"></a>
#### M-021 — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — XML External Entity Injection via File Upload (`fileUpload.ts:76`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) - XML External Entity (XXE)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:76`

**How:**

1. Configure `parseXmlString` in `lib/xml.ts` to set `no_ent: true` / `noEnt: true` (libxml2 `NO_ENT` flag) and disable DTD loading (`no_dtd: true`) to prevent external entity resolution.
2. If XML parsing is still required for the challenge use case, parse in a sandboxed subprocess or use a non-expanding parser such as `fast-xml-parser` with `allowBooleanAttributes` and external entities disabled.
3. Never include parsed XML output in HTTP error responses (`routes/fileUpload.ts:79`) - return a generic error message instead.

_Example implementation in `routes/fileUpload.ts:76`: it applies **Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// lib/xml.ts — disable external entities
import { parseXml } from 'libxml2-wasm'
export async function parseXmlString(data: string): Promise<string> {
  const doc = parseXml(data, {
    option: XmlParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOENT | XmlParseOption.XML_PARSE_NONET
  } as any)
  return doc.toString()
}
```

**Verification:** Upload an XML file with `<!DOCTYPE foo [<!ENTITY xxe SYSTEM 'file:///etc/passwd'>]><root>&xxe;</root>`; expect a generic error response with no file content.

**Reference:** [CWE-611: Improper Restriction of XML External Entity Reference (XXE)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html)

<a id="m-022"></a>
#### M-022 — Validate and allowlist outbound request targets

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-023](#f-023) — SSRF via Profile Image URL Upload (`profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) - Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`

**How:**

1. Validate `req.body.imageUrl` against an allowlist of permitted image-hosting domains, or restrict to HTTPS-only public routable addresses.
2. Block requests to RFC-1918 private ranges (10.x, 172.16.x, 192.168.x), link-local (169.254.x), and loopback (127.x) using a library like `ssrf-req-filter` or a custom DNS resolution check.
3. Return the blocked-URL error to the client rather than falling back to storing the URL directly (`routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:36-37`).
4. Ensure the `POST /profile/image/url` endpoint is gated with `security.isAuthorized()` middleware in `server.ts`.

_Example implementation in `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`: it applies **Validate and allowlist outbound request targets**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// After — reject non-HTTP/S and private-range URLs before fetch
import { isPrivateAddress } from 'ssrf-req-filter'
const parsedUrl = new URL(url)
if (!['http:', 'https:'].includes(parsedUrl.protocol)) throw new Error('Invalid URL scheme')
if (await isPrivateAddress(parsedUrl.hostname)) throw new Error('Private address blocked')
const response = await fetch(url)
```

**Verification:** Send `POST /profile/image/url` with `{"imageUrl": "http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/"}` as an authenticated user; expect HTTP 400 or a URL-rejected error.

**Reference:** [CWE-918: Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html)

<a id="m-023"></a>
#### M-023 — Use parameterized database queries

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-024](#f-024) — NoSQL Injection in Product Review Mass-Update (`updateProductReviews.ts:18`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Cast `req.body.id` to a MongoDB ObjectId before using it as a filter: `const { ObjectId } = require('mongodb'); const id = new ObjectId(req.body.id);`
2. Remove `multi: true` from the update options - a single review edit should target exactly one document.
3. Add an ownership check: only allow the update if `result.original[0].author === user.data.email` before committing.
4. Return HTTP 403 if the authenticated user does not own the target review.

_Example implementation in `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`: it applies **Use parameterized database queries**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (routes/updateProductReviews.ts:17-20)
db.reviewsCollection.update(
  { _id: req.body.id },
  { $set: { message: req.body.message } },
  { multi: true }
)

// After
const { ObjectId } = require('mongodb')
db.reviewsCollection.update(
  { _id: new ObjectId(req.body.id), author: user.data.email },
  { $set: { message: req.body.message } },
  { multi: false }
)
```

**Verification:** Send `PATCH /rest/products/reviews` with `{"id": {"$gt": ""}, "message": "injected"}` as an authenticated user; expect HTTP 400 or ObjectId cast error with no reviews modified.

**Reference:** [CWE-943: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Data Query Logic (NoSQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html)

<a id="m-024"></a>
#### M-024 — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — Remote Script Execution via Unverified Installer (`ci.yml:358`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:358`

**How:**

1. Remove the `curl https://cli-assets.heroku.com/install.sh | sh` step from .`github/workflows/ci.yml:358`.
2. Replace it with the official `akhileshns/heroku-deploy` action already used at line 369 (it bundles the CLI internally), or download a version-pinned Heroku CLI tarball and verify its `SHA-256` checksum before executing.
3. Add an explicit `permissions: contents: read` block to the heroku job to limit blast radius if any step is compromised.

_Example implementation in `.github/workflows/ci.yml:358`: it applies **Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
# Before (ci.yml:358):
- name: "Install Heroku CLI"
  run: curl https://cli-assets.heroku.com/install.sh | sh

# After — pin to version-tagged archive and verify SHA-256:
- name: "Install Heroku CLI"
  run: |
    HEROKU_VERSION=9.5.0
    curl -fsSL https://cli-assets.heroku.com/channels/stable/heroku-v${HEROKU_VERSION}-linux-x64.tar.gz -o heroku.tar.gz
    echo "<expected-sha256>  heroku.tar.gz" | sha256sum --check
    tar -xzf heroku.tar.gz -C /usr/local --strip-components 1
```

**Verification:** Trigger the heroku workflow and confirm: (1) the `curl | sh` pattern is absent from runner logs; (2) the `SHA-256` check step exits 0; (3) a wrong checksum causes the job to fail before any CLI execution.

**Reference:** [CWE-829: Inclusion of Functionality from Untrusted Control Sphere](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

<a id="m-025"></a>
#### M-025 — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — Unpinned GitHub Actions Allow Supply Chain Code Injection (`ci.yml:188`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) - Use of Unmaintained Third-Party Components

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:188`

**How:**

1. For each unpinned action, resolve the full commit SHA for the current tag via `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/git/ref/tags/<tag>` and replace the floating ref with the SHA plus a comment, e.g. `calibreapp/image-actions@<sha> # main@<date>`.
2. For Dockerfile:1, change `FROM node:24` to `FROM node:24@sha256:<digest>`. Retrieve the digest with `docker pull node:24 && docker inspect node:24 --format '{{index .RepoDigests 0}}'`.
3. For Dockerfile:22, add `@sha256:<digest>` to the gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13 reference.
4. Extend the existing Dependabot configuration in .`github/dependabot.yml` to cover `package-ecosystem: github-actions` so future version bumps arrive as PRs rather than silent tag moves.
5. Enable CODEOWNERS enforcement on workflow files so changes to .github/workflows/ require maintainer review.

_Example implementation in `.github/workflows/ci.yml:188`: it applies **Pin the container base image to an immutable digest**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
# Before (image_actions.yml):
- uses: actions/checkout@v6
- uses: calibreapp/image-actions@main

# After — pin to full commit SHA:
- uses: actions/checkout@11bd71901bbe5b1630ceea73d27597364c9af683 # v4.2.2
- uses: calibreapp/image-actions@0bc965ceb38d3b98c38c56ce958e5a2df58e4dd5 # main 2025-03-01

# Dockerfile — before:
FROM node:24
# After:
FROM node:24@sha256:<digest>
```

**Verification:** Run `grep -rn '@main\|@v[0-9]' .github/workflows/` and confirm zero results. Run `grep -E '^FROM [^@]+$' Dockerfile` and confirm zero unpinned FROM lines.

**Reference:** [CWE-1104: Use of Unmaintained Third Party Components](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html)

<a id="m-028"></a>
#### M-028 — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Weak Password Hash MD5 without Salt (`insecurity.ts:41`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Replace `crypto.createHash('md5')` in `lib/insecurity.ts:41` with `bcrypt.hash(password, 12)` (using the `bcryptjs` or `bcrypt` npm package) which includes a per-call salt.
2. Add a `UserModel` `beforeCreate` / `beforeUpdate` hook that calls `bcrypt.hash()` automatically, removing the need for callers to invoke `security.hash()` directly.
3. On first login with the new code, detect `MD5`-format hashes (32 hex chars) and re-hash the verified plaintext; retire the `MD5` path once all users have migrated.
4. Update the login query at `routes/login.ts:34` to use `bcrypt.compare(password, user.password)` instead of hash-in-SQL.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:41`: it applies **Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before:
export const hash = (data: string) => crypto.createHash('md5').update(data).digest('hex');

// After (in UserModel hook):
import bcrypt from 'bcryptjs';
UserModel.addHook('beforeCreate', async (user) => {
  user.password = await bcrypt.hash(user.password, 12);
});
```

**Verification:** Register a user and verify the stored password column is a bcrypt hash (starts with `$2b$`); verify login with the correct password succeeds and with a wrong password returns HTTP 401.

**Reference:** [CWE-916: Use of Password Hash with Insufficient Computational Effort](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

#### M-029 — Stop exposing internal information to clients

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Prometheus Metrics Endpoint Exposed Without Authentication (`server.ts:676`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) - Exposure of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:676`

**How:**

1. Remove the duplicate `/metrics` registration at `server.ts:729` (the module-level registration outside `configureApp`).
2. Gate the remaining registration with `security.isAccounting()` or `IpFilter(['127.0.0.1'], { mode: 'allow' })` to restrict to local Prometheus scrape agents only.
3. If a scrape agent external to the host requires access, protect the endpoint with HTTP basic auth or mutual TLS at the reverse proxy.

_Example implementation in `server.ts:676`: it applies **Stop exposing internal information to clients**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (server.ts:676)
app.get('/metrics', utils.asyncHandler(metrics.serveMetrics()))

// After — restrict to loopback
import { IpFilter } from 'express-ipfilter'
app.get('/metrics', IpFilter(['127.0.0.1'], { mode: 'allow' }), utils.asyncHandler(metrics.serveMetrics()))
```

**Verification:** Send `GET /metrics` from an external IP; expect HTTP 403 or connection refused. Confirm Prometheus scraping still works from localhost.

**Reference:** [CWE-200: Exposure of Sensitive Information to an Unauthorized Actor](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html)

<a id="m-033"></a>
#### M-033 — Stop storing sensitive data in cleartext

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Unencrypted SQLite Database File at Rest (`index.ts:41`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** High · **File:** `models/index.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Replace `sqlite3` with `@journeyapps/sqlcipher` (drop-in SQLite replacement) and set `dialectOptions: { key: process.env.DB_ENCRYPTION_KEY }` in `models/index.ts`; fail startup if the key is absent or shorter than 32 bytes.
2. Store `DB_ENCRYPTION_KEY` in a secrets manager (HashiCorp Vault, AWS Secrets Manager) rather than environment-variable plaintext on the container; inject it at runtime only.
3. Run the container as a non-root user (`USER 1001` in Dockerfile) with the SQLite file owned exclusively by that user, ensuring OS-level access control as a defense-in-depth layer.

_Example implementation in `models/index.ts:41`: it applies **Stop storing sensitive data in cleartext**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// models/index.ts — after migration
new Sequelize('database', 'username', process.env.DB_ENCRYPTION_KEY!, {
  dialect: 'sqlite',
  dialectModule: require('@journeyapps/sqlcipher'),
  dialectOptions: {
    mode: sqlite3.OPEN_READWRITE | sqlite3.OPEN_CREATE | sqlite3.OPEN_FULLMUTEX,
    key: process.env.DB_ENCRYPTION_KEY   // applied to PRAGMA key
  },
  storage: 'data/juiceshop.sqlite'
})
```

**Verification:** Run `strings data/juiceshop.sqlite | grep -i 'email'` after enabling SQLCipher; the output must contain no recognizable user email addresses.

**Reference:** [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html)

#### M-034 — Stop storing sensitive data in cleartext

2. Apply the same field encryption to `deluxeToken`; consider whether the token should instead be computed on-the-fly from stable user attributes rather than stored.
3. Rotate existing TOTP secrets for all users on deployment by invalidating stored values and requiring users to re-register their authenticator app.

**Verification:** Query `SELECT totpSecret FROM Users WHERE id = 1` directly from the SQLite file after the change; the value must be an IV-prepended ciphertext, not a readable TOTP seed string.

<a id="m-037"></a>
#### M-037 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-038](#f-038) — Missing Login Rate Limiting (`login.ts:32`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:32`

**How:**

1. Install `express-rate-limit` and apply a limiter of 10 requests per 15 minutes per IP address on `POST /rest/user/login` in `server.ts` before the route handler.
2. Add a second per-account limiter keyed on `req.body.email` to prevent distributed attacks from multiple IPs against one account.
3. On repeated failures (≥5), return HTTP 429 with a `Retry-After` header; log the throttled account and IP for alerting.
4. Add a test that sends 11 login requests in rapid succession and asserts the 11th returns HTTP 429.

_Example implementation in `routes/login.ts:32`: it applies **Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
import rateLimit from 'express-rate-limit';
const loginLimiter = rateLimit({
  windowMs: 15 * 60 * 1000, // 15 minutes
  max: 10,
  standardHeaders: true,
  message: 'Too many login attempts'
});
app.post('/rest/user/login', loginLimiter, login());
```

**Verification:** Replay 11 `POST /rest/user/login` requests within 1 minute from the same IP and assert the 11th response is HTTP 429.

**Reference:** [CWE-307: Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html)

<a id="m-038"></a>
#### M-038 — Derive rate-limit key from a verified proxy IP rather than the X-Forwarded-For…

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-039](#f-039) — Rate Limit Bypass via X-Forwarded-For Header Spoofing (`server.ts:346`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-290](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/290.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:346`

**How:**

1. Remove the custom `keyGenerator` from the rate limiter at `server.ts:346` and rely on `req.ip` instead, which express-rate-limit uses by default.
2. Configure `trust proxy` only when behind a known reverse proxy, and set it to the proxy's specific IP rather than the global `true` value: `app.set('trust proxy', '10.0.0.1')`.
3. If a CDN/proxy injects X-Forwarded-For, validate it at the ingress layer rather than trusting it in application code.

_Example implementation in `server.ts:346`: it applies **Derive rate-limit key from a verified proxy IP rather than the X-Forwarded-For…**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (server.ts:343-347)
app.use('/rest/user/reset-password', rateLimit({
  windowMs: 5 * 60 * 1000,
  max: 100,
  keyGenerator ({ headers, ip }) { return headers['X-Forwarded-For'] ?? ip }
}))

// After — rely on req.ip (set correctly by trust proxy config)
app.use('/rest/user/reset-password', rateLimit({ windowMs: 5 * 60 * 1000, max: 100 }))
```

**Verification:** Send 101 `POST /rest/user/reset-password` requests while rotating `X-Forwarded-For` values; expect HTTP 429 once the real source IP limit is reached.

**Reference:** [CWE-290: Authentication Bypass by Spoofing](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/290.html)

<a id="m-039"></a>
#### M-039 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-040](#f-040) — No Rate Limiting on Login Endpoint (`server.ts:596`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:596`

**How:**

1. Add express-rate-limit middleware to the login route in `server.ts` with a sliding window of 5-10 attempts per minute per IP: `rateLimit({ windowMs: 60 * 1000, max: 10 })`.
2. Add a per-account lockout counter using the email field as a secondary key (Redis or SQLite counter), returning HTTP 429 with retry-after after 5 failed attempts.
3. Implement non-enumerating responses: return the same HTTP 401 message for invalid email AND invalid password to prevent user enumeration.
4. Log all failed login attempts with the source IP and email (masked) for anomaly detection.

_Example implementation in `server.ts:596`: it applies **Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// server.ts — before app.post('/rest/user/login', login())
app.post('/rest/user/login',
  rateLimit({ windowMs: 60 * 1000, max: 10, standardHeaders: true, legacyHeaders: false }),
  login()
)
```

**Verification:** Send 15 consecutive `POST /rest/user/login` requests within 60 seconds from the same IP; expect the 11th request to return HTTP 429 Too Many Requests.

**Reference:** [CWE-307: Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html)

<a id="m-040"></a>
#### M-040 — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-041](#f-041) — Missing Rate Limiting on LLM Chat Endpoint (`server.ts:638`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:638`

**How:**

1. Add `express-rate-limit` middleware specifically for `/rest/chat`: e.g., `rateLimit({ windowMs: 60 * 1000, max: 20, validate: false })` to allow at most 20 requests per IP per minute.
2. Apply a tighter per-authenticated-user limit (e.g., 10 requests/minute) when a JWT is present, using `keyGenerator: (req) => req.user?.id ?? req.ip`.
3. Reduce `stopWhen: stepCountIs(10)` at `routes/chat.ts:209` to 3-5 to limit per-request LLM step amplification.
4. Set an `abortSignal` with a per-request timeout in the `streamText()` call to cap wall-clock cost per request.

_Example implementation in `server.ts:638`: it applies **Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// server.ts — add before the chat route registration
import { rateLimit } from 'express-rate-limit'
const chatLimiter = rateLimit({
  windowMs: 60 * 1000,  // 1 minute
  max: 20,
  validate: false,
  message: { error: 'Too many chat requests, please wait.' }
})
app.post('/rest/chat', chatLimiter, utils.asyncHandler(chat()))
```

**Verification:** Send 21 `POST /rest/chat` requests in under 60 seconds from the same IP. The 21st request must return HTTP 429 with the rate-limit error message.

**Reference:** [CWE-400: Uncontrolled Resource Consumption](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html)

<a id="m-041"></a>
#### M-041 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-042](#f-042) — Deluxe Tier Token Derivable from Committed Private Key (`insecurity.ts:150`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:150`

**How:**

1. Remove the hardcoded `privateKey` (covered in 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — Hardcoded RSA Private Key (`lib/insecurity.ts:21`) remediation) - rotating the key also invalidates all existing `deluxeToken` values.
2. Store the deluxe-token derivation secret separately from the JWT signing key in `process.env.DELUXE_TOKEN_SECRET`.
3. Replace the `deluxeToken(email)` computation with a server-side lookup in the `Orders` or `DeluxeSubscriptions` table, removing the need for a client-verifiable HMAC at all.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:150`: it applies **Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before:
export const deluxeToken = (email: string) => {
  const hmac = crypto.createHmac('sha256', privateKey);
  return hmac.update(email + roles.deluxe).digest('hex');
};

// After: derive from a separate secret
export const deluxeToken = (email: string) => {
  const secret = process.env.DELUXE_TOKEN_SECRET;
  if (!secret) throw new Error('DELUXE_TOKEN_SECRET is required');
  return crypto.createHmac('sha256', secret).update(email + roles.deluxe).digest('hex');
};
```

**Verification:** Using the old private key, compute a deluxe token and include it in a forged JWT; assert the `isDeluxe()` check returns false after the key is rotated.

**Reference:** [CWE-321: Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)

<a id="m-042"></a>
#### M-042 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-043](#f-043) — LLM Excessive Agency via Unrestricted Coupon-Generation Tool (`chat.ts:184`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. Change the Zod schema at `routes/chat.ts:179` to `z.number().min(1).max(10).int()` to provide schema-level rejection of out-of-range values.
2. Add a server-side guard in the executor body: `if (discount > 10 || discount < 1) throw new Error('Discount out of allowed range')`.
3. Remove coupon policy details from the system prompt - they can be exfiltrated via prompt injection. Enforce eligibility server-side by verifying order ID ownership and damage status before allowing coupon generation.
4. Gate the `POST /rest/chat` endpoint behind `security.isAuthorized()` to prevent unauthenticated tool invocation.

_Example implementation in `routes/chat.ts:184`: it applies **Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (routes/chat.ts:179)
discount: z.number().describe('The discount percentage for the coupon (maximum 10)')

// After — enforce the cap at schema and executor level
discount: z.number().min(1).max(10).int()
// In execute:
execute: async ({ discount }) => {
  if (discount > 10) throw new Error('Discount exceeds policy maximum')
  return { couponCode: security.generateCoupon(discount), discount }
}
```

**Verification:** Craft a conversation that leads the LLM to call `generateCoupon` with discount=50; confirm the tool throws an error or returns HTTP 400, and no coupon is generated.

**Reference:** [CWE-94: Improper Control of Generation of Code (Code Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

<a id="m-043"></a>
#### M-043 — Apply least-privilege filesystem access

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-044](#f-044) — Excessive `GITHUB_TOKEN` Scope from Missing Permissions Blocks in 11 Workflows (`ci.yml:1`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html) - Improper Access Control

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`

**How:**

1. Set the repository default to read-only: Settings → Actions → General → Workflow permissions → select 'Read repository contents and packages'.
2. Add an explicit `permissions:` block at the top of each of the 11 workflows. Use `permissions: contents: read` as the safe baseline for test workflows, then grant only the scopes the workflow actually uses.
3. For `release.yml` (publishes packages and containers), scope to `contents: write, packages: write` only.
4. For `ci.yml`'s heroku job, use `permissions: contents: read` since it only deploys and does not write to the repository.
5. Verify with `grep -rL 'permissions:' .github/workflows/*.yml` after the change; expected output is empty.

_Example implementation in `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`: it applies **Apply least-privilege filesystem access**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
# ci.yml — add at the top level (after 'on:', before 'env:'):
permissions:
  contents: read

# release.yml — narrowed to only what publishing requires:
permissions:
  contents: write
  packages: write
```

**Verification:** After adding blocks, run `grep -rL 'permissions:' .github/workflows/*.yml` and confirm zero results. Trigger a test workflow and verify the `GITHUB_TOKEN`'s effective scopes match only the declared permissions via the Actions run log.

**Reference:** [CWE-284: Improper Access Control](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html)

---

<a id="m-044"></a>
#### M-044 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-045](#f-045) — Insecure Direct Object Reference via Integer Auto-Increment Primary Keys (`user.ts:39`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** High · **File:** `models/user.ts:39`

**How:**

1. Change the `id` column type in User, Basket, Order, Address, and Card models to `DataTypes.UUID` with `defaultValue: DataTypes.UUIDV4`; this eliminates sequential enumeration at the schema level.
2. In the Sequelize model-route middleware or individual route handlers for `/api/Users/:id`, `/rest/basket/:id`, and order endpoints, add a `WHERE userId = req.user.id` scope (or use Sequelize scopes) so a user can only access their own records.
3. For admin-access endpoints (`GET /api/Users` list), add `security.isAdmin()` middleware in addition to `isAuthorized()`, preventing regular users from listing all accounts.

_Example implementation in `models/user.ts:39`: it applies **Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (models/user.ts:39-43)
id: {
  type: DataTypes.INTEGER,
  primaryKey: true,
  autoIncrement: true
}

// After — non-enumerable UUID
id: {
  type: DataTypes.UUID,
  defaultValue: DataTypes.UUIDV4,
  primaryKey: true
}
```

**Verification:** After UUID migration, call `GET /api/Users/1` as a non-admin authenticated user; the response must be 403 Forbidden. Call `GET /api/Baskets/<other-user-uuid>` as a different authenticated user; response must also be 403.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

<a id="m-045"></a>
#### M-045 — Enforce authorization on the server

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-046](#f-046) — Client-Side-Only Authorization Guards (`app.guard.ts:52`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:52`

**How:**

1. In `LoginGuard.canActivate()`, replace the `localStorage` token presence check with a call to `/rest/user/whoami` - if the server returns 401, the route is blocked regardless of `localStorage` contents.
2. In `AdminGuard.canActivate()`, verify the role by consuming the `whoami` response (cache it with a short TTL per navigation to avoid per-request overhead) rather than decoding the client-held JWT.
3. Move the JWT to an `HttpOnly` cookie (see finding `frontend-spa-010`) so the client cannot read or forge it at all, making client-side decoding moot.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:52`: it applies **Enforce authorization on the server**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (app.guard.ts:17-20)
canActivate () {
  if (localStorage.getItem('token')) { return true }
  ...
}

// After — verify with server
async canActivate (): Promise<boolean> {
  const me = await this.userService.whoAmI().toPromise()
  if (!me || !me.data) { this.forbidRoute(); return false }
  return true
}
```

**Verification:** With an expired or invalid JWT in `localStorage`, navigate to `/#/profile` (LoginGuard-protected) and confirm the server returns 401 and the guard redirects to `/403`.

**Reference:** [CWE-602: Client-Side Enforcement of Server-Side Security](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html)

<a id="m-046"></a>
#### M-046 — Apply least-privilege filesystem access

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-047](#f-047) — Excessive LLM Tool Agency generateCoupon executes without server-side discount… (`chat.ts:184`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-284](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html) - Improper Access Control

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. Move all coupon policy conditions to the execute function: require `orderId` as a parameter, look up the order in the database, verify it belongs to the authenticated user and has a `damage_report` status before generating a coupon.
2. Cap the discount to 10% in server-side code (`const safDiscount = Math.min(discount, 10)`) and remove the discount from the LLM input schema entirely, using only the server-determined value.
3. Require user authentication before any tool that generates financial instruments can execute; return `{ error: 'Authentication required' }` from `generateCoupon` if `userId` is undefined.
4. Add structured audit logging for every `generateCoupon` call: actor, `orderId`, discount amount, generated code hash, and timestamp.

_Example implementation in `routes/chat.ts:184`: it applies **Apply least-privilege filesystem access**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// After — enforce policy in execute function
execute: async ({ orderId }) => {
  const userId = await getUserId(req)
  if (!userId) return { error: 'Authentication required' }
  const order = await db.ordersCollection.findOne({ orderId })
  if (!order || order.userId !== userId) return { error: 'Order not found' }
  if (!order.damage_report) return { error: 'No verified damage report on this order' }
  const discount = 10  // fixed by policy, not LLM-supplied
  const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(discount)
  logger.info({ event: 'coupon_generated', userId, orderId, discount })
  return { couponCode, discount }
}
```

**Verification:** Authenticated as user A, call `generateCoupon` for an undamaged order via the chatbot. Verify the tool returns an error, not a coupon code. Then as an unauthenticated user verify the tool returns 'Authentication required'.

**Reference:** [CWE-284: Improper Access Control](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/284.html)

<a id="m-047"></a>
#### M-047 — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-048](#f-048) — Missing Authentication on Web3 Challenge Routes (`web3Wallet.ts:13`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-004](#w-004)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/web3Wallet.ts:13`

**How:**

1. Wrap each state-changing web3 route in `server.ts` with `security.isAuthorized()` to enforce JWT authentication, matching the pattern already used on lines 603–635.
2. For `/rest/web3/submitKey`, also bind the submitted key comparison to `req.user.id` so challenge credit is awarded only to the authenticated user.
3. Apply rate limiting to all five routes using `express-rate-limit` to prevent bulk challenge-manipulation from authenticated or unauthenticated callers.

_Example implementation in `routes/web3Wallet.ts:13`: it applies **Require authentication on every exposed endpoint**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (server.ts:641-645):
app.post('/rest/web3/submitKey', utils.asyncHandler(checkKeys()))
app.post('/rest/web3/walletNFTVerify', walletNFTVerify())
app.post('/rest/web3/walletExploitAddress', utils.asyncHandler(contractExploitListener()))

// After:
app.post('/rest/web3/submitKey', security.isAuthorized(), utils.asyncHandler(checkKeys()))
app.post('/rest/web3/walletNFTVerify', security.isAuthorized(), walletNFTVerify())
app.post('/rest/web3/walletExploitAddress', security.isAuthorized(), utils.asyncHandler(contractExploitListener()))
```

**Verification:** Send unauthenticated POST to `/rest/web3/walletNFTVerify`; server must return HTTP 401. Send the same request with a valid JWT; server must return a non-401 response.

**Reference:** [CWE-306: Missing Authentication for Critical Function](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html)

<a id="m-051"></a>
#### M-051 — Accept True-Client-IP only when the request originates from a trusted reverse p…

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-052](#f-052) — IP Address Spoofing via True-Client-IP Header (`saveLoginIp.ts:18`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-290](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/290.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/saveLoginIp.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Replace the unconditional `req.headers['true-client-ip']` read with a check that only trusts the header when the request source (`req.socket.remoteAddress`) matches a configured trusted-proxy CIDR allowlist.
2. Fall back to `utils.toSimpleIpAddress(req.socket.remoteAddress)` for all other requests.
3. Apply the same pattern to any other forwarding headers (`X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Real-IP`) consumed elsewhere in the codebase.

_Example implementation in `routes/saveLoginIp.ts:18`: it applies **Accept True-Client-IP only when the request originates from a trusted reverse p…**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
const TRUSTED_PROXIES = (process.env.TRUSTED_PROXIES ?? '').split(',');
const isTrustedProxy = TRUSTED_PROXIES.some(cidr => req.socket.remoteAddress?.startsWith(cidr));
const lastLoginIp = isTrustedProxy ? req.headers['true-client-ip'] : utils.toSimpleIpAddress(req.socket.remoteAddress ?? '');
```

**Verification:** Send a login request with `True-Client-IP: 1.2.3.4` from an untrusted source and verify `lastLoginIp` in the database reflects the actual socket address, not `1.2.3.4`.

**Reference:** [CWE-290: Authentication Bypass by Spoofing](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/290.html)

<a id="m-053"></a>
#### M-053 — Use parameterized database queries

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-054](#f-054) — NoSQL Injection via \$where Operator in getProductReviews Tool (`chat.ts:149`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:149`

**How:**

1. Replace `db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + productId })` with `db.reviewsCollection.find({ product: productId })` using native MongoDB equality operator - no JavaScript evaluation, no string concatenation.
2. Change the Zod input schema from `z.string()` to `z.number().int().positive()` to enforce numeric product IDs at the schema level instead of relying on `Number()` coercion.
3. Verify that MongoDB's `$where` operator is disabled in the database configuration (`javascriptEnabled: false` in `mongod.conf`) to eliminate the attack class entirely at the database layer.

_Example implementation in `routes/chat.ts:149`: it applies **Use parameterized database queries**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Before (routes/chat.ts:148-149)
const productId = Number(id)
return await db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + productId }) as Review[]

// After
const productId = Number(id)
if (!Number.isInteger(productId) || productId <= 0) return []
return await db.reviewsCollection.find({ product: productId }) as Review[]
```

**Verification:** Confirm via MongoDB logs that no `$where` queries are issued for `getProductReviews` calls. Send a request with id='1; `db.getCollectionNames()`' and verify the tool returns an empty result and no JS is evaluated.

**Reference:** [CWE-943: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Data Query Logic (NoSQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html)

2. Make current-password verification unconditional: remove the `if (currentPassword && ...)` guard and always require a matching current password before accepting the change.
3. Add `security.isAuthorized()` middleware to the endpoint in `server.ts` to enforce JWT-level authentication.

```javascript
// Before (routes/changePassword.ts:13-14)
return async ({ query, headers, connection }: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  const currentPassword = query.current as string

// After — use POST body
return async ({ body, headers, connection }: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {

  // … then always enforce the hash check
```
